Django 6.2 in development (6.2.dev20260926051415), at the commit 4fab678a0739d54401ccee7eb587553657c9f76e, on Python 3.14 with SQLite 3.50.4, on Windows,
and a copy of the project harbour made for the run, with a fourth application, yard, whose models change as the recording goes.
The formatter black is not on the path: Django runs it on each migration file it writes when it is.

The run's directory as the recording begins, every file in it: the copy of harbour, and what the script has added to it, which is manage.py, settings.py and harbour/yard/__init__.py; no application has a migrations directory, and there is no database file
    manage.py
    settings.py
    harbour/__init__.py
    harbour/routers.py
    harbour/serials.py
    harbour/crew/__init__.py  (empty)
    harbour/crew/models.py
    harbour/fleet/__init__.py  (empty)
    harbour/fleet/models.py
    harbour/office/__init__.py  (empty)
    harbour/office/models.py
    harbour/yard/__init__.py  (empty)

What a Migration instance holds: fleet.0001_initial as the loader made it from its file, and crew.0002_initial, the second migration the first makemigrations wrote for crew
    loader = MigrationLoader(None)
    first = loader.disk_migrations['fleet', '0001_initial']
    type(first)  ->  harbour.fleet.migrations.0001_initial.Migration
    type(first).__mro__[1]  ->  django.db.migrations.migration.Migration
    str(first)  ->  'fleet.0001_initial'
    repr(first)  ->  '<Migration fleet.0001_initial>'

suggest_name: `initial` for a migration marked initial; else the operations' name fragments joined, as many as stay within 52 characters, then `_and_more`; and `auto_` with the date and the time where an operation has no fragment or there is no operation
    first.suggest_name()  ->  'initial'
    second.suggest_name()  ->  'initial'
    loader.graph.nodes['yard', '0001_initial'].suggest_name()  ->  'initial'
    # migrations of the script's own, each made by `made` from the operations given it:
      def made(*operations, initial=None, **attributes):
          """A migration of the script's own, of the application yard, with these operations."""
          from django.db.migrations import Migration
          migration = Migration("made", "yard")
          migration.operations = list(operations)
          migration.initial = initial
          for name, value in attributes.items():
              setattr(migration, name, value)
          return migration
    metres = migrations.AddField('dock', 'metres', models.PositiveSmallIntegerField(default=120))
    longer = migrations.AlterField('dock', 'name', models.CharField(max_length=80))
    begun = migrations.RenameField('repair', 'started', 'begun')
    by_dock = migrations.AddIndex('repair', models.Index(fields=['dock', 'begun'], name='repair_by_dock'))
    made(metres).suggest_name()  ->  'dock_metres'
    made(metres, longer).suggest_name()  ->  'dock_metres_alter_dock_name'

    made(begun, metres, longer).suggest_name()  ->  'rename_started_repair_begun_dock_metres_and_more'
    made(begun, metres, longer, by_dock).suggest_name()  ->  'rename_started_repair_begun_dock_metres_and_more'
    made(by_dock).suggest_name()  ->  'repair_repair_by_dock'
    made(migrations.DeleteModel('Dock')).suggest_name()  ->  'delete_dock'
    made(migrations.RunPython(migrations.RunPython.noop)).suggest_name()  ->  'auto_(the date)_(the time)'

    made().suggest_name()  ->  'auto_(the date)_(the time)'

_build_migration_list at the first makemigrations: the migrations in the order they were cut from the lists of operations (each line Migration.__init__ is one being made), and what each holds before the optimizer; crew's operations are cut in two: its second migration begins at the first operation that needs a model of fleet's or office's, whose own migrations need crew's Sailor, and holds every operation after that one too

A model renamed (to-states of the script's own, in which Dock is Basin): with a questioner that says yes, a RenameModel; with one that says no, the old model deleted and the new one created; the question is put only where the two models' fields are the same
    # changes of the script's own are put to the autodetector by `detect`:
      def detect(*, before=(), after=(), questioner=None, **answers) -> Said:
          """What the autodetector makes of a change of the script's own. The from-state is the one
          the loader makes from the migration files and the to-state the one ProjectState.from_apps
          makes from the models; the state halves of the operations in `before` are run on both, to
          set the scene, and those in `after` on the to-state alone, which is the change. The answer
          is each migration that changes() returns, with its operations."""
          from django.apps import apps
          from django.db.migrations.autodetector import MigrationAutodetector
          from django.db.migrations.loader import MigrationLoader
          from django.db.migrations.questioner import MigrationQuestioner
          from django.db.migrations.state import ProjectState
          loader = MigrationLoader(None)
          from_state, to_state = loader.project_state(), ProjectState.from_apps(apps)
          for operation in before:
              operation.state_forwards("yard", from_state)
              operation.state_forwards("yard", to_state)
          for operation in after:
              operation.state_forwards("yard", to_state)
          autodetector = MigrationAutodetector(from_state, to_state, questioner or MigrationQuestioner(defaults=answers))
          changes = autodetector.changes(graph=loader.graph)
          return Said("; ".join(f"{migration}: [{', '.join(operation_detail(operation) for operation in migration.operations)}]" for migrations in changes.values() for migration in migrations) or "no changes")
    detect()  ->  no changes
    detect(after=[migrations.RenameModel('Dock', 'Basin')], ask_rename_model=True)  ->  yard.0002_rename_dock_basin: [RenameModel: Rename model Dock to Basin]
    detect(after=[migrations.RenameModel('Dock', 'Basin')], ask_rename_model=False)  ->  yard.0002_basin_alter_repair_dock_delete_dock: [CreateModel: Create model Basin (fields id, name, port), AlterField: Alter field dock on repair (ForeignKey(on_delete=PROTECT, to='yard.basin')), DeleteModel: Delete model Dock]
    detect(after=[migrations.RenameModel('Dock', 'Basin'), migrations.AlterField('basin', 'name', models.CharField(max_length=80))], ask_rename_model=True)  ->  yard.0002_basin_alter_repair_dock_delete_dock: [CreateModel: Create model Basin (fields id, name, port), AlterField: Alter field dock on repair (ForeignKey(on_delete=PROTECT, to='yard.basin')), DeleteModel: Delete model Dock]
    detect(after=[migrations.RenameModel('Dock', 'Basin'), migrations.AddField('basin', 'metres', models.PositiveSmallIntegerField(default=120))], ask_rename_model=True)  ->  yard.0002_basin_alter_repair_dock_delete_dock: [CreateModel: Create model Basin (fields id, name, metres, port), AlterField: Alter field dock on repair (ForeignKey(on_delete=PROTECT, to='yard.basin')), DeleteModel: Delete model Dock]

The interactive questioner, with answers typed ahead: the questions answered yes or no
    # an interactive questioner, given a prompt_output that writes into this recording as `stdout` lines, as makemigrations gives it its own standard output; and for the process's standard input a stand-in of the script's own, from which input() reads the lines the script types ahead:
      class Keyboard(io.TextIOBase):
          """What the interactive questioner reads its answers from in this part, in place of the
          process's standard input: the lines the script has typed ahead, each written as a line
          `stdin` of the recording as input() reads it."""
          def __init__(self):
              self.lines = []
          def readline(self, size: int = -1) -> str:
              if not self.lines:
                  return ""
              line = self.lines.pop(0)
              if LINES and LINES[-1].endswith(NOT_ENDED):  # the prompt: the line typed ends its line
                  LINES[-1] = LINES[-1][: -len(NOT_ENDED)]
                  ASKING["out"].open_line = False
              LINES.append("  " * len(STACK) + f"stdin  | {line}")
              return line + "\n"
    asking = InteractiveMigrationQuestioner(prompt_output=OutputWrapper(out))

      stdin  | y

    keyboard.lines = ['']

      stdin  |
    answer  ->  False
    keyboard.lines = ['perhaps', 'No']

      stdout | Please answer yes or no:
      stdin  | No
    answer  ->  False

reduce, pair by pair: what an operation answers when the optimizer asks whether the operation after it can be folded into it: a list of the operations to put in place of the two, True if the second may be moved across it, False if it may not
    # operations of the script's own; in this part an operation is written with what tells it from another of its class
    create = migrations.CreateModel('Crane', [('id', models.BigAutoField(primary_key=True))])
    tonnes = migrations.AddField('crane', 'tonnes', models.PositiveSmallIntegerField(default=5))
    heavier = migrations.AlterField('crane', 'tonnes', models.PositiveIntegerField(default=5))
    heaviest = migrations.AlterField('crane', 'tonnes', models.BigIntegerField(default=5))
    load = migrations.RenameField('crane', 'tonnes', 'load')
    burden = migrations.RenameField('crane', 'load', 'burden')
    no_tonnes = migrations.RemoveField('crane', 'tonnes')
    hoist = migrations.RenameModel('Crane', 'Hoist')
    options = migrations.AlterModelOptions('crane', {'ordering': ['id']})
    by_tonnes = migrations.AddIndex('crane', models.Index(fields=['tonnes'], name='crane_by_tonnes'))
    no_index = migrations.RemoveIndex('crane', 'crane_by_tonnes')
    light = migrations.AddConstraint('crane', models.CheckConstraint(condition=models.Q(tonnes__lt=100), name='crane_is_light'))
    no_light = migrations.RemoveConstraint('crane', 'crane_is_light')
    no_crane = migrations.DeleteModel('Crane')
    metres = migrations.AddField('dock', 'metres', models.PositiveSmallIntegerField(default=120))
    on_dock = migrations.AddField('crane', 'dock', models.ForeignKey('yard.Dock', models.CASCADE))
    sql = migrations.RunSQL('UPDATE yard_dock SET name = name')
    passing = migrations.RunSQL('UPDATE yard_dock SET name = name', elidable=True)

              self.number = number

The signals: migrate run in this process applies yard's second migration; pre_migrate is sent to each application before anything is applied, with the registry of the state the applied migrations make, and post_migrate after, with the registry of the state the executor returned
    # a receiver of the script's own:
      def watching(sender, **kwargs):
          """A receiver of the script's own, connected to pre_migrate and to post_migrate for the block:
          it writes down what it is given, in full where the sender is yard's AppConfig."""
          from django.apps import apps as main_registry
          name = SIGNAL_NAMES[id(kwargs["signal"])]
          if sender.label != "yard":
              note(f"watching(sender={brief(sender)}, ...)  for {name}")
              return
          note(f"watching(sender={brief(sender)}, ...)  for {name}, with the keyword arguments {', '.join(sorted(kwargs))}")
          note(f"  app_config is sender: {kwargs['app_config'] is sender}; using: {kwargs['using']!r}; verbosity: {kwargs['verbosity']!r}; interactive: {kwargs['interactive']!r}")
          note(f"  plan: {whole(kwargs['plan'])}")
          registry = kwargs["apps"]
          note(f"  apps: {brief(registry)}; apps is the main registry: {registry is main_registry}")
          note(f"  the fields of apps.get_model('yard', 'Repair'): {[field.name for field in registry.get_model('yard', 'Repair')._meta.fields]!r}")
    pre_migrate.connect(watching)
    post_migrate.connect(watching)
    call_command('migrate', stdout=out)

sqlmigrate, as command lines: the statements sqlmigrate collects for a migration, forwards or backwards, each operation under a comment that names it; an operation that sends nothing has (no-op) under its name; where a table is remade, its columns need not stand in the order migrate gives them, which the block on sqlmigrate's calls shows

The data migration's file: harbour/yard/migrations/0004_name_the_foremen.py after the script has written two functions and two operations into the file makemigrations --empty made, as a developer would by hand
    | # Generated by Django 6.2.dev20260926051415 on (the date and the time)
    |
    | from django.db import migrations
    |
    |
    | def name_foremen(apps, schema_editor):
    |     """Every repair whose foreman was not recorded is put down to the yard's master."""
    |     Repair = apps.get_model("yard", "Repair")
    |     Repair.objects.using(schema_editor.connection.alias).filter(foreman="not recorded").update(foreman="Yard master")
    |
    |
    | def forget_foremen(apps, schema_editor):
    |     Repair = apps.get_model("yard", "Repair")
    |     Repair.objects.using(schema_editor.connection.alias).filter(foreman="Yard master").update(foreman="not recorded")
    |
    |
    | class Migration(migrations.Migration):
    |
    |     dependencies = [
    |         ('yard', '0003_repair_foreman'),
    |     ]
    |
    |     operations = [
    |         migrations.RunPython(name_foremen, reverse_code=forget_foremen),
    |         migrations.RunSQL(
    |             sql="UPDATE yard_repair SET notes = 'No notes were kept.' WHERE notes = ''",
    |             reverse_sql="UPDATE yard_repair SET notes = '' WHERE notes = 'No notes were kept.'",
    |         ),
    |     ]

An operation that cannot be reversed: Migration.unapply raises IrreversibleError in its first pass, before any database half has run; RunPython's own database_backwards raises NotImplementedError
    # a function of the script's own, and a migration of the script's own whose RunPython is given no reverse_code:
      def count_repairs(apps, schema_editor):
          """A function of the script's own for a RunPython: it counts the repairs, with the historical model."""

RunPython in a transaction or not (with the statements as SQLite ran them, `sqlite`): in a migration that is not atomic, the function runs with no transaction begun unless the RunPython was made with atomic=True, for which Migration.apply runs its database half in an atomic block
    # two migrations of the script's own that are not atomic, each with one RunPython: one made with atomic left alone, one with atomic=True; each is applied by hand, as apply_migration applies a migration, to the state the loader makes of every migration, rendered first
    loader = MigrationLoader(None)
    state = loader.project_state()
    state.apps  # rendered here, as the executor renders its state before it applies a migration
    connection.ensure_connection()  # opened here, so that what a connection sends as it opens is not among the lines below
    alone = made(migrations.RunPython(count_repairs), atomic=False)
    guarded = made(migrations.RunPython(count_repairs, atomic=True), atomic=False)

    guarded.operations[0].atomic  ->  True
    connection.schema_editor(atomic=alone.atomic).atomic_migration  ->  False
    with connection.schema_editor(atomic=alone.atomic) as schema_editor: state = alone.apply(state, schema_editor)
